TASK: Find the root cause of a 256 MB/step GPU memory leak in the C++ NativeDynamicShapePlan slot execution during autoregressive decode.

PROJECT: /home/agibsonccc/Documents/GitHub/deeplearning4j

CONFIRMED FACTS:
1. The leak is 256 MB per decode step, confirmed by per-step GPU free memory tracking
2. Java only receives 1 output (logits) per step — the leak is entirely C++ internal
3. The model has 2743 ops. kvScatterInCpp=true. shapesFrozen_=true. GEM_SLOT_BY_SLOT mode.
4. `writeOutputSlot()` in NativeDynamicShapePlan.cpp (line 260-334) already has code to free old arrays when replaced
5. BUT: at line 1428-1429 of NativeDynamicShapePlan_slotexec.cpp, there's a condition `slotArrayCache_ != outputSlots_` that is ALWAYS FALSE because `#define slotArrayCache_ outputSlots_` (header line 1702). This means the `delete oldCached` at line 1431 NEVER executes.
6. DSP in isolation (small models) does NOT leak. Only large models (2743 ops) through StaticKvCacheDecodeLoop leak.
7. `releaseGpuIntermediates()` is NOT called between decode steps.
8. cudaMemPoolTrimTo recovers only 32MB — this is a TRUE allocation leak.

KEY SUSPICION: 
In the frozen execution path of `executeSlot()` (NativeDynamicShapePlan_slotexec.cpp around line 1364-1440):
- Line 1394: `ctxOuts[i]->nullify()` nullifies the EXISTING output before op execution
- Line 1417: `slot.ident.op->execute(&ctx)` executes the op, which allocates NEW output arrays
- Line 1435: `writeOutputSlot(si, ctxOuts[i], "frozen-op-exec")` writes the new array
- `writeOutputSlot` should free the old array via planOwnedArrays_ check
- BUT: if the old array was nullified at line 1394, it may no longer be the same pointer

The nullify at line 1394 is suspicious — it operates on `ctxOuts[i]` which is `ctx.fastpath_out()[i]`. This is the op context's output array from the PREVIOUS execution. When nullified, its data buffer is released. Then `op->execute()` allocates a NEW data buffer. But the NDArray OBJECT itself might be reused (same pointer, new buffer). If writeOutputSlot sees `old == value` (same NDArray pointer), it won't free anything — but the OLD data buffer was already released by nullify.

OR: The op might create an entirely NEW NDArray object (new pointer). In that case writeOutputSlot should free the old. But if the old was already nullified (and its buffer freed), deleting it might double-free. So maybe the code intentionally avoids deletion.

FILES TO INVESTIGATE:
1. `libnd4j/include/graph/impl/NativeDynamicShapePlan_slotexec.cpp` — executeSlot() frozen path (lines 1300-1500)
2. `libnd4j/include/graph/impl/NativeDynamicShapePlan.cpp` — writeOutputSlot() (lines 255-334) 
3. `libnd4j/include/graph/NativeDynamicShapePlan.h` — the `#define slotArrayCache_ outputSlots_` at line 1702
4. `libnd4j/include/ops/declarable/DeclarableOp.cpp` — how ops allocate outputs during execute()

QUESTION TO ANSWER:
1. When a frozen op executes, does it REUSE the existing NDArray* output (same pointer, same buffer) or allocate a NEW NDArray* with a new buffer?
2. If it allocates new, does writeOutputSlot correctly free the old one?
3. Is the `slotArrayCache_ != outputSlots_` check at line 1428 the dead code that prevents freeing?
4. What is the ACTUAL allocation pattern — trace from `op->execute()` to where the output NDArray is created

RULES:
- NEVER use git checkout, git stash, git reset --hard, or git clean — BANNED
- DO NOT modify any files — research only
- Report specific line numbers and the exact leak mechanism

  1224	void DeclarableOp::overwriteResult(Context &block, int outputIdx, NDArray *array, bool remove) {
  1225	  if (block.isFastPath()) {
  1226	    auto existing = block.fastpath_out()[outputIdx];
  1227	    if (block.shapeFunctionOverride() && existing != nullptr && existing != array) {
  1228	      // DSP mode: Java pre-allocated the output and owns its DataBuffer.
  1229	      // We must NOT delete it (Java still has a reference).
  1230	      // Copy data from the new array if shapes match.
  1231	      if (existing->isSameShape(array) && existing->dataType() == array->dataType()) {
  1232	        existing->assign(array);
  1233	        delete array;
  1234	        return;
  1235	      }
  1236	      // Shapes differ — fall through but NEVER delete the Java-managed output
  1237	      remove = false;
  1238	    }
  1239	    if (remove && existing != nullptr) {
  1240	      sd_debug("Deleting extra reference in fast path at idx %d\n",outputIdx);
  1241	      delete existing;
  1242	    }
  1243	    sd_debug("In fast path, setting variable\n", 0);
  1244	    block.fastpath_out()[outputIdx] = array;
  1245	  } else if (block.getVariableSpace() == nullptr) {
  1246	    THROW_EXCEPTION("Var space should not be null before pushing variable!");
  1247	  } else {
  1248	    block.pushNDArrayToVariableSpace(block.nodeId(), outputIdx, array, remove);
  1249	    sd_debug("After pushing variable\n", 0);
  1250	    auto varSpace = block.getVariableSpace();
  1251	    if (varSpace == nullptr) {
  1252	      THROW_EXCEPTION("Var space should not be null!");
  1253	    }
  1254	    sd_debug("After getting var space\n", 0);
  1255	    if (varSpace->hasVariable(block.getNodeId(), outputIdx)) {
  1256	      sd_debug("calling get variable\n", 0);
  1257	      auto var = varSpace->getVariable(block.getNodeId(), outputIdx);
  1258	      sd_debug("after calling get variable", 0);
  1259	      if (var->getNDArray() != nullptr && var->isRemovable()) delete var->getNDArray();
  1260	
  1261	      var->setNDArray(array);
  1262	      var->markRemovable(true);
  1263	    } else {
  1264	      sd_debug("Creating new variable\n", 0);
  1265	      auto var = new Variable(array, nullptr, block.getNodeId(), outputIdx);
  1266	      varSpace->putVariable(block.getNodeId(), outputIdx, var);
  1267	      sd_debug("Putting variable\n", 0);
  1268	    }
  1269	  }

  1271	
  1272	void DeclarableOp::overwriteResult(Context &block, int outputIdx, NDArray *array) {
  1273	  if (block.isFastPath()) {
  1274	    auto existing = block.fastpath_out()[outputIdx];
  1275	    if (block.shapeFunctionOverride() && existing != nullptr && existing != array) {
  1276	      // DSP mode: Java pre-allocated the output and owns its DataBuffer.
  1277	      // Replacing the pointer orphans the Java-managed NDArray and leaks the
  1278	      // C++-created temporary (tZ) since Java never sees the replacement.
  1279	      // Instead, copy data from tZ into the existing output if shapes match.
  1280	      if (existing->isSameShape(array) && existing->dataType() == array->dataType()) {
  1281	        existing->assign(array);
  1282	        delete array;  // Free the C++-created temporary
  1283	        return;
  1284	      }
  1285	      // Shapes differ — DSP shape computation didn't match the op's actual output.
  1286	      // Log once and fall through to replacement (will leak, but avoids data corruption).
  1287	      static bool warnedOnce = false;
  1288	      if (!warnedOnce) {
  1289	        auto existingShape = ShapeUtils::shapeAsString(existing);
  1290	        auto newShape = ShapeUtils::shapeAsString(array);
  1291	        sd_printf("WARNING: OVERWRITE_RESULT in DSP mode with shape mismatch: existing=%s new=%s (op output idx %d). "
  1292	                  "This leaks the C++ temporary array. Fix DSP shape computation for this op.\n",
  1293	                  existingShape.c_str(), newShape.c_str(), outputIdx);
  1294	        warnedOnce = true;
  1295	      }
  1296	    }
  1297	    block.fastpath_out()[outputIdx] = array;
  1298	  } else {
  1299	    auto varSpace = block.getVariableSpace();
  1300	    if (varSpace == nullptr) {
  1301	      THROW_EXCEPTION("Var space should not be null in overwriteResult!");
  1302	    }
  1303	    block.pushNDArrayToVariableSpace(block.nodeId(), outputIdx, array);
  1304	    if (varSpace->hasVariable(block.getNodeId(), outputIdx)) {
  1305	      auto var = varSpace->getVariable(block.getNodeId(), outputIdx);
  1306	      if (var->getNDArray() != nullptr && var->isRemovable()) delete var->getNDArray();
  1307	
  1308	      var->setNDArray(array);
  1309	      var->markRemovable(true);
  1310	    } else {
  1311	      auto var = new Variable(array, nullptr, block.getNodeId(), outputIdx);
  1312	      varSpace->putVariable(block.getNodeId(), outputIdx, var);
  1313	    }
  1314	  }
  1315	}

   598	bool sd::ops::DeclarableOp::allocateResult(Context &block, sd::LongType *shape) {
   599	  auto var = block.variable(block.getNodeId(), 0);
   600	
   601	  auto workspace = block.getWorkspace();

   603	  sd::LongType len = shape::length(shape);

   609	  // if that's first run - we probably have nothing here
   610	  if (var->getNDArray() == nullptr) {
   611	    auto shapeInfo = ConstantShapeHelper::getInstance().bufferForShapeInfo(__shape)->primary();
   612	    RELEASE(__shape, workspace);
   613	    DataBuffer * buffer = nullptr;
   614	#ifdef __cpp_exceptions
   615	    try {
   616	      buffer = new DataBuffer(len * sizeof(int8_t), ArrayOptions::dataType(shapeInfo), workspace);
   617	      var->setNDArray(new NDArray(buffer, shapeInfo, block.launchContext()));
   618	    } catch (...) {
   619	      // Clean up buffer if NDArray construction fails
   620	      if (buffer != nullptr) {
   621	        delete buffer;
   622	      }
   623	      throw;
   624	    }
   625	#else
   626	    buffer = new DataBuffer(len * sizeof(int8_t), ArrayOptions::dataType(shapeInfo), workspace);
   627	    var->setNDArray(new NDArray(buffer, shapeInfo, block.launchContext()));
   628	#endif
   629	  } else if (var->getNDArray()->lengthOf() != len) {
   630	    // if length not match - lets reallocate array
   631	    delete var->getNDArray();
   632	    auto shapeInfo = ConstantShapeHelper::getInstance().bufferForShapeInfo(__shape)->primary();
   633	    DataBuffer * buffer = nullptr;
   634	#ifdef __cpp_exceptions
   635	    try {
   636	      buffer = new DataBuffer(len * sizeof(int8_t), ArrayOptions::dataType(shapeInfo), workspace);
   637	      var->setNDArray(new NDArray(buffer, shapeInfo, block.launchContext()));
   638	    } catch (...) {
   639	      // Clean up buffer if NDArray construction fails
   640	      if (buffer != nullptr) {
   641	        delete buffer;
   642	      }
   643	      RELEASE(__shape, workspace);
   644	      throw;
   645	    }
   646	#else

   659	void sd::ops::DeclarableOp::DeclarableOp::traceExecIfNeeded(Context &block) {
   660	  if(OpRegistrator::getInstance().traceOps()) {
   661	    std::vector<const LongType *> *inputShapeBuffers = new std::vector<const LongType *>();
   662	    for(size_t i = 0; i < block.width(); i++) {
   663	      inputShapeBuffers->push_back(block.variable(i)->getNDArray()->shapeInfo());
   664	    }
   665	    std::vector<const LongType *> *outputShapeBuffers = new std::vector<const LongType *>();
   666	    for(size_t i = 0; i < block.outputWidth(); i++) {
   667	      outputShapeBuffers->push_back(block.fastpath_out()[i]->shapeInfo());
   668	    }
   669	
   670	    OpExecTrace *opExecTrace = new OpExecTrace(inputShapeBuffers,outputShapeBuffers, getOpName());
   671	    OpRegistrator::getInstance().registerOpExec(opExecTrace);
   672	  }
   673	}

   685	    // if length not match - lets reallocate array
   686	    delete var->getNDArray();
   687	    var->setNDArray(new NDArray(order, shape2, block.dataType(), block.launchContext()));
   688	  }
   689	
   690	  return true;
   691	}

  1152	  // Pre-execute setup: clear stale errors, manage attention workspace,
  1153	  // flush pending close, invalidate stale cached graphs.

  1158	  // Step 1: Initialize output slots
  1159	  // When shapes are frozen (after warmup), pre-populate from slotArrayCache_ so
  1160	  // downstream ops can read inputs without each slot individually setting outputSlots_.
  1161	  // View-producer slots will be overwritten during execution.
  1162	  //
  1163	  // Non-capturable (and permanently capture-failed) segments execute slot-by-slot
  1164	  // across decode steps. Their shape-driving scalar tensors often keep the same
  1165	  // shape while values change (KV length growth), so cross-execution shape cache
  1166	  // reuse can become stale and cause later broadcast mismatches. Invalidate these
  1167	  // segment-local caches each execute; capturable graph-replay segments keep caches.
  1168	
  1169	  // replayManagedSlots REMOVED: arrays persist (one array per slot), no protection needed.
  1170	
  1171	  // Build protected weight buffer set (once on first execute).
  1172	  // DataBuffers from external constants/variables must NEVER be freed. View ops
  1173	  // can produce intermediate NDArrays that share a DataBuffer with a model weight.
  1174	  // Without this protection, freeing the intermediate frees the weight's GPU memory
  1175	  // → CUDA illegal access (error 700) on re-execution.
  1176	  // This mirrors the Java-side protectedWeightBuffers in DynamicShapePlanExecutor.
  1177	  if (protectedWeightBuffers_.empty()) {
  1178	    for (int i = 0; i < numExternalInputs; i++) {
  1179	      if (externalInputs[i] != nullptr) {
  1180	        auto* db = externalInputs[i]->dataBuffer();
  1181	        bool isVariableInput = i < static_cast<int>(externalInputIsVariable_.size()) &&
  1182	                               externalInputIsVariable_[i];
  1183	        if (db != nullptr && !isVariableInput) {
  1184	          protectedWeightBuffers_.insert(db);
  1185	        }

libnd4j/include/graph/Context.h:68:  bool _shapeFunctionOverride = false;
libnd4j/include/graph/impl/Context.cpp:950:void Context::setShapeFunctionOverride(bool reallyOverride) { _shapeFunctionOverride = reallyOverride; }
libnd4j/include/graph/impl/Context.cpp:952:bool Context::shapeFunctionOverride() { return _shapeFunctionOverride; }

codex
The context flag is sticky: `setShapeFunctionOverride(true)` is set in the normal DSP path and I don’t see any reset to `false`. That means later frozen executions enter `DeclarableOp::execute()` with shape override still enabled, so the generic output allocator is bypassed and the op normally receives the existing `fastpath_out()` pointers.
